Um... yeah, sleep has been a little tricky. I really don't want to complain, because I know everyone gets tired, right? But... I guess I've barely been sleeping since it happened. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... startled easily? Well, I mean, as a courier, you kind of always have to stay on your toes, right? I've always prided myself on being really alert and steady behind the wheel. I definitely don't want you to think I'm a nervous wreck or an unsafe driver now or anything like that. 

But, um... I guess maybe my reflexes are just a little, I don't know, a little too dialed in right now? It's actually kind of embarrassing, honestly. Lately, if my dispatch radio crackles unexpectedly, or a police siren goes off, or even if someone just slams a car door nearby... I just jump. Like, I physically jump, like I'm about to get rushed all over again. 

And then my heart just starts pounding so incredibly hard. It gets so intense that I actually have to pull the van over for a minute, or step outside if I'm in the middle of dropping off a package, just to catch my breath and get it to stop. I always make sure I pull over safely, I promise! I'm still being really responsible about my job. But... that's a normal reaction, isn't it? Just my body being a little overly cautious after a scare? I really hope it goes away soon so I can just be back to my old self.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... in a daze? Spaced out? Well, I mean, I really try to stay sharp, you know? Especially with driving all day and keeping track of the manifests. I definitely don't want you to think I'm just zoning out behind the wheel or ignoring my responsibilities. I take my job very seriously.

But, um... no, I don't think I get spaced out or anything. I mean, maybe I get a little tired at the end of a long shift, like anyone would, but I'm always paying attention to what I'm doing. I haven't been wandering around in a daze or anything like that. I'm keeping my focus where it needs to be. That's what you're supposed to do, right? Just keep pushing through and staying alert? I really hope that means I'm handling everything okay.

Um... avoiding it? Well, I mean, I wouldn't really call it avoiding, exactly. I just... I found a different route that I prefer. Well, I mean, I try to just focus on my work, you know? I want to be a reliable courier, so I try to just keep my mind on the job. I don't want you to think I'm running away from my problems or anything like that. \n\nBut, um... I guess I have been making a few little changes to my routes lately. Nothing major, I promise! Just... well, I've been turning down the night runs. And, um, I've been kind of taking the long way around town so I don't have to drive anywhere near that detention building. Like, I physically jump, like I'm about to get rushed all over again. \n\nAnd then my heart just starts pounding so incredibly hard. Just my body being a little overly cautious after a scare? I really hope it goes away soon so I can just be back to my old self.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself, or like things aren't real?\n\nPatient: Um... outside of my body?
